Because #one-one + #one-zero + #zero-one + #zero-zero = n-m. Total ones n/2. For small m, #one-one? The average over m of R_m (both ones) = (S(n-S))/(2(n-1))? For small m, if R_m average n/8, then zeros-zero also n/8, total pairs (same values) n/4, transitions n/2, average c_m+c_-m=n/2 each? But we need each c ≈n/8, sum ≈n/4, not n/2. Wait total c over all offsets = one-zero ordered pairs S(n-S)≈n^2/4 over 2n offsets average n/8. For each m positive, c_m+c_-m average over m? Total positive+negative for m>0 = two offsets. Average c_m+c_-m over m>0 = (2*(n-1)*average each?)/? Total average over all offsets = n/8, so average sum = n/4. So need for each m, total transitions = n/4, i.e., same-value pairs =3n/4. For large small ignore. c_d (positive) = ∑x_i(1-x_{i+d}) = ∑x_i - R = (n-d)/2 - R. In y terms, c_d = (n-d)/4 - (1/4)∑ y_i y_{i+d}? plus edge? How to implement reweighted projected gradient efficiently:
function reweight_optimize(h0, weights_init, steps, step_sizes, beta, budget_deadline):
   h = project(h0)
   best_h, best_score = h, score_fft(h)
   w = softmax over c_offset? We can define weights from c: c_off = c[::-1]; temp maybe β/(scale+small). To avoid weights saturated, use softmax with temperature dynamically: temp = max(max_c - min_c?)/p. We can simply use w = np.exp(β * c_off / max_c) / sum. But c values up to n/2; β small. Use β=1, temperature = max_c * 0.5? e.g., exp(c_off / (max_c+1e-9)). This emphasizes larger c. Or use rank-based weights: top M active with weights proportional to c. Dense softmax over all lags (O(n)) each update, fine.
   Iterate:
     If time left.
     c_off = correlate_offset(h) (fft)
     sc = max(c_off)*2/n
     If exact? maybe FFT.
     Update best if sc < best_score (with final exact occasionally)
     Update weights: if sc too high? w = softmax(c_off / T). T maybe max(1e-6, 0.1 * sc * n/2?) Actually c units; use T = 0.25*max_c + eps to not too sharp. Or use p-norm gradient: weights = (c_off / sum c)^p? Let's use temperature adaptive: T = 0.1 * max_c; if max_c - p95 small, T = (max_c-p95)*5? Simpler: w = exp((c_off - max_c)/T); T = max(1e-9, 0.2*max_c). Then weights sum maybe over many lags. For n=1000, 2000 lags, exp(-large/T) decays. Good.
     grad = weighted_gradient(h,w)
     Step: choose step_size. Use backtracking line search? J weighted not max. We can do fixed small steps with armijo on max objective? Since we optimize max, need accept if max decreases after several steps. Could use projected gradient on surrogate: minimize F=weighted sum. But we accept based on actual max. Use line search: for candidate, project, score. Try steps: 0.01,0.5,0.2,0.1,...? Gradient scale can be large (w sum 1, grad entries O(1)?). h range [0,1]. Step initial maybe 0.1. Use backtracking with Armijo on actual max? Need gradient of max not surrogate. But if w emphasizes active lags, gradient of surrogate approximates subgradient of max. We can just do stochastic descent on actual max using subgradient at active lags: w uniform over active (top threshold). Then gradient is subgradient. Use subgradient method with decreasing step. But max subgradient is non-smooth; can still.
   Maybe simpler: Use "max-min" iterative: At each iteration, set weights uniform over lags with c > (1-ε)max. Then minimize weighted sum with a few gradient steps. This is like Merrick? Good.

Subgradient method details:
- active lags: c_off >= thresh = max_c - delta (delta maybe 0.05*max_c or fixed). w=1/count over active.
- gradient = weighted_gradient(h,w) (uniform active).
- h_new = project(h - eta * grad)
- Choose eta. For subgradient, need diminishing eta. But we can use line search to maximize decrease in max: try eta in list [1e-4,3e-4,1e-3,3e-3,1e-2,3e-2] maybe. Score FFT for each (line search expensive: up to 6 objective evals per step). Could do a couple.
- Or use fixed eta=0.05 with smoothing and restarts.

Total update when flip position p:
For all m such that either i=p or i+m=p. Let p as first index i=p, m such that p+m in [0,n): p as second index i=p-m, m such that p-m in [0,n). We can update each c_{p,q}? More directly, c_m can be seen as sum over ordered pairs (i,j) with j-i=m of x_i(1-x_j). Flipping x_p affects pairs where p is i (term x_p(1-x_j)) and where p is j (term x_i(1-x_p)).
- If x_p changes 0->1:
  For pairs p as i (m=j-p): before term 0, after (1-x_j). c_m += 1 - x_j for all j≠p? m valid (j from p+? any m such that j=p+m in range). For m including 0: before 0, after 0 (since 1-1=0) but term p,p x_p(1-x_p) is 0 both; our formula 1-x_p for j=p =0, okay. So for m from -(n-1-p) to (n-1-p), c_m += 1 - x_{p+m}.
  For pairs p as j (m=p-i): before x_i*1, after x_i*0 => c_m -= x_i for i=p-m valid. m from p-(n-1) to p. Including m=0 before 0 after0? x_p before 0? For 0->1, x_p before 0, after1; term x_p(1-x_p) before0, after0; formula -x_i with i=p? x_p before 0, so -0=0, fine. For m where i=p? m=0; if we already did p as i, no double? At m=0, same pair (p,p); update from 0->1 total 0. Our two updates: as i add 1-x_p=0; as j subtract x_p=0. Good.
  Need avoid double? For m where both i and j? only p=p m=0 handled.
- If x_p changes 1->0:
  As i: before 1-x_j, after 0 => c_m -= 1-x_j = -(1-x_j)
  As j: before x_i, after x_i*(1-0)=x_i? Wait term x_i(1-x_j) before x_j=1 =>0? Actually if p as j and x_p=1 before, term x_i(0)=0; after x_p=0, term x_i(1)=x_i => +x_i. So c_m += x_i.

Thus update formula:
def apply_flip_inplace(x,c,p):
  old = x[p]
  s = 1 if old==0 else -1
  ns = n-1
  # Need x old before change. x is int array.
  if s==1:
     c[ns-p : 2*ns-p+1] += (1 - x)  # length n
     c[p : n+p] -= (x[::-1])
  else:
     c[ns-p : 2*ns-p+1] -= (1 - x)
     c[p : n+p] += (x[::-1])
  x[p] = 1-old
  c[ns] -= 1
But does c[ns] -=1 always? For n=1? n even >. If c[ns] might go negative due rounding? It should remain 0. For old=0, slice1 at q=ns adds 1, slice2 at q=ns adds 0, subtract ->0. For old=1, slice1 0, slice2 +1, subtract ->0. Good.

Need ensure x[::-1] uses old x; we change x[p] after. Good. Score max(c) maybe for binary c nonnegative. c could have -0? ok.

Weighted gradient implementation details:
def weighted_gradient(h, w_off, n):
  # w_off length 2n-1, float64 maybe sum 1.
  # If w dense, use FFT. But if w is sparse? We can just always FFT. Need next pow2 for 3n-2.
  N = 1 << (3*n - 2 - 1).bit_length()? For L=3n-2, need N>=L. L-1 bit.
  fh = np.fft.rfft(h, N)
  S = np.fft.irfft(fh * np.fft.rfft(w_off[::-1], N), N)
  S = S[n-1 : 2*n - 2]? i 0..n-1 start n-1 end n-1+n =2n-1 exclusive? Since start n-1, length n -> stop 2n-1. (indices n-1..2n-2)
  B = np.fft.irfft(fh * np.fft.rfft(w_off, N), N)
  B = B[n-1 : 2*n - 1]
  Wwin via cumsum:
    cum = np.empty(2n)?? w_off length 2n-1. We can do np.cumsum(np.concatenate(([0], w_off))) each call O(n). Or precompute window sums for current w. w changes each iteration.
    cums = np.cumsum(w_off); cums = np.insert(cums,0,0)
    idx = np.arange(n)
    Wwin = cums[2*n-1 - idx] - cums[n-1 - idx]
  return Wwin - S - B
Check S, B index: For n=2, L=4, N=4? h len2 w len3 conv length4. S=some. S slice [1:3] length2; B [1:3]. Good.
But N should be >=3n-2=4; next pow2(4)=4. rfft length4 okay. However product of length4 gives cyclic convolution. Need N>=L to avoid wrap. L=4, N=4 okay full length? Linear conv length 4 with N=4 exactly no alias? For cyclic conv, if N >= len(a)+len(b)-1 = 4, okay, result first 4 coefficients. Good.

Projection bisection alternative: Since h and z values in [0,1] plus step, sum near target. We can do:
def project_sum_bounds(z, target):
  n=len(z)
  y = np.clip(z,0,1)
  diff = target - y.sum()
  if abs(diff) < 1e-12: return y
  # if diff >0 add to entries with z>0? Use waterfilling
  # Use bisection t in [min(z)-1, max(z)+1] maybe 30 iter.
  lo = np.min(z) - 1.0
  hi = np.max(z) + 1.0
  for _ in range(30):
    mid=(lo+hi)/2
    s=np.clip(z+mid,0,1).sum()
    if s < target: lo=mid else hi=mid
  return np.clip(z+(lo+hi)/2,0,1)

def project_fast(z,target):
  y=np.clip(z,0,1)
  diff=target-y.sum()
  if diff==0 return y
  if diff>0:
    # add to positions with y<1, proportional slack
    slack=1-y
    total=slack.sum()
    if total<=0: return y
    add=diff/total
    y += add*slack
    y=np.clip(y,0,1)
    # if not exact, bisection few iters
  else:
    excess=y
    total=excess.sum()
    sub=-diff/total
    y -= sub*excess
    y=np.clip(y,0,1)
  # If sum error >1e-10, bisection 8 iters starting from z=y? Actually y now close.
  if abs(y.sum()-target)>1e-9:
    # bisection on z? Use original z? Maybe use y as z.
    y = project_bisection(y,target, iters=8)
  return y

But exact via one variable if slack enough. Since diff small if z close; can add diff to first entry with slack. But numerical exact maybe. Let's do:
def project_simple(z,target):
  y=np.asarray(z,float)
  y=np.clip(y,0,1)
  diff=target-y.sum()
  if abs(diff)>1e-12:
    if diff>0:
       slack=1-y
       idx=np.where(slack>0)[0]
       if len(idx):
          # distribute uniformly? Choose max slack index; but if diff small, can put into one.
          # To maintain simplicity: y[idx[0]] += diff clipped; if over, adjust? Need exact.
          # Better scaled add over all slack, then clip, then fix largest.
          y += diff/len(idx)
       y=np.clip(y,0,1)
       diff=target-y.sum()
       if abs(diff)>1e-12:
          # fix one entry
          if diff>0:
             idx=np.argmax(1-y)
             y[idx]=min(1,y[idx]+diff)
          else:
             idx=np.argmax(y)
             y[idx]=max(0,y[idx]+diff)
  return y

def smooth_minimize(h0, beta_start, beta_end, lr, max_evals, deadline):
  h = project_fast(h0,target)
  state Adam m,v
  for beta in annealing sequence (or increase over steps):
    current G,score
    for step in range(max_evals or time):
       G, grad, score = smooth_value_grad(h,beta)
       update best if score < best_score (FFT) or exact occasionally
       Adam update
       h = project_fast(h - stepsize*adam, target)
       maybe line search: if new_score > old_score + 0.01 for several steps, reduce step size / random restart.

      ns=n-1
      if x[p]==0:
        c[ns-p : 2*ns-p+1] += y
        c[p : n+p] -= x[::-1]
      else:
        c[ns-p : 2*ns-p+1] -= y
        c[p : n+p] += x[::-1]
      x[p] ^= 1
      y[p] ^= 1
      c[ns] -= 1

Now continuous smooth_minimize implementation details:
Function smooth_minimize(h0,n,time_limit,deadline,rng,beta_max maybe,lr):
  target=n/2.0
  h = project_fast(np.clip(h0,0,1), target)
  best_h=h.copy(); best_score=fft_score(h); best_G maybe.
  m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
  t=0
  lr0=0.05
  beta_sequence maybe depending on phase: [1,2,5,10,20,40]. We can allocate time slices equal across betas? Use loop over betas, within until deadline.
  For each beta:
     # reset Adam? maybe divide m,v by 2? Keep.
     prev_score=best_score
     while time<deadline:
        G, grad, score = smooth_value_grad(h,beta)
        # update best
        if score < best_score - 1e-12: best_h=h.copy(); best_score=score
        t +=1
        m = 0.9*m +0.1*grad? use beta1=0.9,beta2=0.999
        v = 0.999*v +0.001*grad*grad
        mhat=m/(1-0.9**t); vhat=v/(1-0.999**t)
        step = lr0 * mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
        h_new = project_fast(h - step, target)
        # optional line search: compute score_new maybe cheap? Already computed G? We can accept if score_new < score + small? But computing score_new needs fft_corr. We can evaluate new score to decide and use for best. That doubles FFT per step. Maybe not; accept blindly, if worsens too much revert.
        # Track if score worsens: we don't know new score until next iter's G. If next score high, may oscillate. We can compute new_score via fft_corr before project? That's one extra fft_corr (N2). Weighted_gradient uses two FFTs. Could evaluate new_score by fft_corr (one rfft pair) maybe 1/3 cost. Let's do to guide step size: score_new = fft_max(h_new) maybe. If score_new > score + 0.02 and not initial, revert h_new = h; reduce lr0 *=0.7; else accept h_new; if score_new < score*0.999 reset lr0 min(0.1,lr0*1.2). Also update best.
        # But score_new computed with FFT, not exact; okay.
        if time...
     if no improvement for 2000 steps (score > best+1e-4), perturb or beta? 
  return best_h,best_score.

Alternative use L-BFGS-B which does line search internally. It may be more robust. Let's use SciPy for continuous and custom for restarts? L-BFGS-B can maximize? It minimizes. Objective smooth. We'll implement a continuous optimizer using scipy.optimize.minimize L-BFGS-B per starting vector and beta. This may be easier and efficient.

L-BFGS-B approach in detail:
- We need top-level objective_grad_u(u, n, beta, lam): uses h=sigmoid(u), project? Sigmoid always in (0,1); sum penalty. No bounds needed.
- Use u0 = logit(h0) clipped [-10,10]. For h0 maybe 0/1; logit of 0/1 infinite; clip to 1e-6.
- β sequence. For each beta, run minimize from current u with maxiter maybe 200, maxfun 500. Use if time remains. After run, h=sigmoid(u); project exact; update best.
- λ penalty: We also minimize (sum-target)^2. Since u unbounded, sum can drift. λ=1000 maybe. But if λ too high, initial u may converge to sum before optimizing. We can schedule λ increasing: [10,100,1000]. Or use constraint? L-BFGS-B no equality. Use penalty. For n=160/320, sum error maybe small. final project may change objective slightly.
- Objective nonconvex; L-BFGS-B stops when gtol. Use finite? We supply jac. Need be careful with beta high: weights few; gradient may be sparse. L-BFGS-B approximate BFGS may struggle. But okay.
- Time per minimize: For n=320, each fun ~ maybe 0.2ms? Actually FFT N corr640, grad1024, maybe 0.3ms; 500 eval=0.15s. Python overhead of scipy maybe more ~0.5ms? 500=0.25s. Many betas/starts possible.

Project_fast: Need good. Let's implement:
def project_fast(z, target):
    n = len(z)
    y = np.clip(z, 0.0, 1.0)
    diff = target - float(np.sum(y))
    if abs(diff) <= 1e-12:
        return y
    # water-fill by bisection only if needed and n not huge? Use 12 iterations on z?
    # Since y is clipped, if diff positive, we can add to entries with highest z within slack; but simple proportional then fix.
    if diff > 0:
       slack = 1.0 - y
       tot = float(np.sum(slack))
       if tot <= 0:
           # all ones but target<n impossible (target n/2), but maybe n=1; return y
           return y
       add = diff / tot
       y += add * slack
       y = np.clip(y,0,1)
       diff = target - float(np.sum(y))
    else:
       tot = float(np.sum(y))
       if tot <= 0: return y
       sub = -diff / tot
       y -= sub * y
       y = np.clip(y,0,1)
       diff = target - float(np.sum(y))
    # final single-entry correction
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
       if diff > 0:
          idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - y))
          y[idx] = min(1.0, y[idx] + diff)
       else:
          idx = int(np.argmax(y))
          y[idx] = max(0.0, y[idx] + diff)
    # if still not exact due bound, distribute over top few
    diff = target - float(np.sum(y))
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
       order = np.argsort((1.0-y) if diff>0 else y)
       step = np.sign(diff) * min(abs(diff)/max(1,len(order)), 1e-12)
       # easier: bisection 8 iter
       for _ in range(8):
          # approximate bisection on z? Use y as z.
          lo = -1.0; hi=1.0
          for __ in range(10):
             mid=(lo+hi)/2
             s=np.clip(y+mid,0,1).sum()
             if s < target: lo=mid
             else: hi=mid
          break
    return y
This is too complex. Simpler: Use bisection on z for final if needed. But project called every step; bisection 12* n heavy. The proportional correction likely enough; final single entry can make sum within 1e-14 if slack enough. If not slack (all y at bounds), target n/2 impossible because y would all 0/1 count target? If diff remains, choose a boundary entry adjust? But at bounds cannot. The proportional step spreads diff and clip; if diff large, maybe still after clip one bound. Single entry may not fix if diff > slack at idx. But target n/2 and z from h - step near prior sum target, diff small. So fine.

Make exact sum for final: We can write adjust_sum_exact(h,target):
  h=np.clip(h,0,1)
  for _ in range(3):
    s=np.sum(h); diff=target-s
    if abs(diff)<1e-13: break
    if diff>0:
       idx=np.argmax(1-h)
       room=1-h[idx]
       if room <=0: break
       h[idx]=min(1,h[idx]+diff)
    else:
       idx=np.argmax(h)
       room=h[idx]
       if room <=0: break
       h[idx]=max(0,h[idx]+diff)
  # One more: set a middle-valued entry to target - sum(others) using np.sum
  idx=int(np.argmax(np.minimum(h,1-h)))
  if 0 <= idx < len(h):
     others = np.sum(np.delete(h, idx))
     val = target - others
     if 0 <= val <= 1:
        h[idx]=val
     # else proportional
  return h

Functions:
def smooth_h_value_grad(h, n, beta):
  target not needed.
  h = np.asarray(h, float)
  # clip? SLSQP bounds; but if h from L-BFGS maybe. Use h = np.clip(h,0,1)
  c = fft_corr(h)
  c_off = c[::-1]
  dx=2.0/n
  s_off = c_off * dx
  # clean
  np.maximum(s_off, 0.0, out=s_off) # modifies copy? c_off view; if modify c? Use s_off = np.maximum(...)
  m = float(s_off.max())
  z = beta * (s_off - m)
  # if beta large, z max 0, min large negative; exp safe. But if z contains nan? no.
  z_max = float(z.max())
  w = np.exp(z - z_max)
  wsum = float(w.sum())
  if wsum <= 0 or not finite: w = np.ones_like(s_off)/len(s_off)
  else: w = w / wsum
  G = m + math.log(wsum)/beta if wsum>0 else m
  grad = dx * weighted_gradient(h, w, n)
  return G, grad
Note weighted_gradient reuses FFT. It will use w normalized. If s_off all zeros (binary? c not all zero except constant? h all 0.5? c? no). If max=0 and wsum number, G=0.

Minimize wrapper:
def slsqp_refine(h0,n,beta,maxiter,deadline):
   global OPT_N, OPT_BETA
   OPT_N=n; OPT_BETA=beta
   x0=project_fast(np.clip(h0,0,1), n/2)
   bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
   cons = [{'type':'eq','fun':_constraint_fun,'jac':_constraint_jac}]
   try:
      res = scipy.optimize.minimize(_slsqp_fun, x0, method='SLSQP', jac=_slsqp_jac, bounds=bounds, constraints=cons, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':maxiter*2,'ftol':1e-10})
   except Exception:
      return x0
   h=res.x
   h=project_fast(h,n/2)
   # check if time already exceeded maybe res still okay.
   return h
But scipy.optimize.minimize may be slow to import? Already allowed. We can import scipy.optimize as scopt.

binary_init_c(x): y=1-x; c=np.correlate(x.astype(np.int32), y.astype(np.int32),'full')[::-1]; return c
binary_local_search(x0,n,deadline,rng,no_improve_threshold=3000,reinit_interval=10000):
   target=n//2
   x = make_binary_from_values? x0 int, adjust sum.
   y=1-x
   c=...
   M=int(c.max()); best_x=x.copy(); best_c=c.copy(); best_M=M
   no_imp=0; it=0; T_bits=2
   start_time=time.time()
   while time.time() < deadline:
      it +=1
      # temperature decay
      T_bits = 2 if it < 2000 else (1 if it < 20000 else 0)
      # generate candidate p (1) q (0)
      p=q=-1
      # active lag
      if M > 0:
        qm = int(np.argmax(c))
        m = qm - (n-1)
        found=False
        if m > 0:
           for _ in range(6):
              i = int(rng.integers(0, n-m))
              if x[i]==1 and x[i+m]==0:
                 p=i; q=i+m; found=True; break
        elif m < 0:
           e=-m
           for _ in range(6):
              i = int(rng.integers(e, n))
              if x[i]==1 and x[i-e]==0:
                 p=i; q=i-e; found=True; break
        if not found:
           # random pair
           for _ in range(10):
              p=int(rng.integers(n)); q=int(rng.integers(n))
              if p!=q and x[p]==1 and x[q]==0: break
           else:
              # find from sorted arrays
              ones=np.where(x==1)[0]; zeros=np.where(x==0)[0]
              if len(ones)==0 or len(zeros)==0: break
              p=int(ones[0]); q=int(zeros[0])
      else:
         break
      # apply flips
      binary_flip(x,y,c,n,p); binary_flip(x,y,c,n,q)
      Mtrial=int(c.max())
      accept=False
      if Mtrial < M:
         accept=True
      elif Mtrial == M and rng.random() < 0.1:
         accept=True
      elif Mtrial < best_M:
         accept=True
      elif T_bits > 0 and Mtrial <= M + T_bits and rng.random() < 0.1:
         accept=True
      if accept:
         M=Mtrial
         if Mtrial < best_M:
             best_M=Mtrial; best_x=x.copy(); best_c=c.copy(); no_imp=0
         else:
             if Mtrial < M? no; no_imp +=1 maybe if not better best
             no_imp += 1
      else:
         # revert
         binary_flip(x,y,c,n,p); binary_flip(x,y,c,n,q)
         no_imp +=1
      if no_imp > 5000 or it % 10000 == 0:
         # perturb from best or random
         x = best_x.copy(); y=1-x; c=best_c.copy(); M=best_M
         # random 20 pair flips regardless
         for _ in range(20):
            p=int(rng.integers(n)); q=int(rng.integers(n))
            if p!=q and x[p]!=x[q]:
               binary_flip(x,y,c,n,p); binary_flip(x,y,c,n,q)
            else:
               # try find
               for __ in range(5): ...
         M=int(c.max())
         no_imp=0
   score = best_M * 2.0 / n
   return best_x, score

Binary initialization from continuous h:
def make_binary_from_continuous(h,n):
   x = (h >= 0.5).astype(np.int8)
   target=n//2
   s=int(x.sum())
   if s>target:
      ones=np.where(x==1)[0]
      vals=h[ones]
      order=np.argsort(vals) # worst ones lowest value first flip to 0
      for i in order[:s-target]: x[i]=0
   elif s<target:
      zeros=np.where(x==0)[0]
      vals=h[zeros]
      order=np.argsort(vals)
      for i in order[s-target:]: x[i]=1 # flip highest zeros? Need correct count: need flip target-s zeros with largest h. order ascending; take last target-s.
   return x
But if we flip in loop, x updates. Good.

Upscale/downsample:
def resample_h(h, n_target):
  if h is None: return None
  n = len(h)
  if n == n_target: return h.copy()
  if n_target % n == 0:
     return np.repeat(h, n_target//n)
  if n % n_target == 0:
     # average blocks
     blocks=n//n_target
     return h.reshape(n_target, blocks).mean(axis=1)  # if n_target divides n; but h length n maybe contiguous.
  # interpolation at centers
  old_x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*(2.0/n)
  new_x=(np.arange(n_target)+0.5)*(2.0/n_target)
  return np.interp(new_x, old_x, h)

Custom Adam/line search for larger n:
We can implement `smooth_line_search_refine(h0,n,time_limit)` that uses weighted_gradient and line search. Add to phases for n in [640,1280] if time. This might find improvements beyond SLSQP. Let's include a simpler version.

Function smooth_line_search_refine(h0,n,deadline,rng):
  target=n/2
  h=project_fast(h0,target)
  best_h=h.copy(); best_score=fft_score(h)
  # beta anneal [2,5,10,20,40]
  for beta in [2,5,10,20,40]:
    # iterations until deadline or stall
    stall=0
    for _ in range(5000):
      if time.time()>=deadline: break
      G, grad, score = smooth_value_grad_score(h,beta,n)
      if score < best_score - 1e-12: best_h=h.copy(); best_score=score
      # normalize gradient
      gnorm = math.sqrt(float(np.sum(grad*grad)))
      if gnorm < 1e-10: break
      direction = -grad / gnorm
      improved=False
      for alpha in [0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005,0.002]:
          h_new = project_fast(h + alpha*direction, target)
          sc_new = fft_score(h_new)
          if sc_new < score - 1e-10:
              # accept if also not much worse than best? yes
              h = h_new; score = sc_new
              if sc_new < best_score - 1e-12: best_h=h.copy(); best_score=sc_new
              improved=True; break
      if not improved:
          stall += 1
          if stall > 200:
             # perturb from best
             h = best_h + rng.normal(0,0.05,n)
             h = project_fast(h,target)
             stall=0
      else:
          stall=0
  return project_exact(best_h,c), best_score

def next_pow2(m):
    m=int(max(1,m))
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()

`weighted_gradient(h,w,n)`:
  L=2*n-1
  N=next_pow2(3*n-2)  # conv h (n) with w(L) length L+n-1=3n-2. For n=1? not.
  fh = np.fft.rfft(h,N)
  # S = conv h with w_rev
  w_rev = w[:-1? No w length L, reversed w[::-1].]
  S = np.fft.irfft(fh * np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], N), N)[:3*n-2]
  S = S[n-1:2*n-1]
  B = np.fft.irfft(fh * np.fft.rfft(w, N), N)[:3*n-2]
  B = B[n-1:2*n-1]
  # Wwin
  cums = np.empty(L+1, float)
  cums[0]=0; cums[1:]=np.cumsum(w)
  idx=np.arange(n)
  W = cums[2*n-1 - idx] - cums[n-1 - idx]
  grad = W - S - B
  # clean small imaginary? irfft real.
  return grad

`project_fast(z,target)`:
Implement robust but not too slow. Let's write:
def project_fast(z, target):
    y = np.clip(np.asarray(z, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    diff = target - float(np.sum(y))
    if abs(diff) > 1e-13:
        if diff > 0:
            slack = 1.0 - y
            tot = float(np.sum(slack))
            if tot > 0:
                y += (diff / tot) * slack
                y = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)
                diff = target - float(np.sum(y))
        else:
            tot = float(np.sum(y))
            if tot > 0:
                y -= (-diff / tot) * y
                y = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)
                diff = target - float(np.sum(y))
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
        # bisection water-fill for correctness, at most 20 iterations
        lo = -1.0
        hi = 1.0
        # ensure sum clip(z + lo) <= target <= sum clip(z+hi)? z may outside. Use min/max z:
        zc = np.asarray(z, dtype=float)
        lo = float(np.min(zc)) - 1.0
        hi = float(np.max(zc)) + 1.0
        for _ in range(25):
            mid = 0.5*(lo+hi)
            s = float(np.sum(np.clip(zc + mid, 0.0, 1.0)))
            if s < target:
                lo = mid
            else:
                hi = mid
        y = np.clip(zc + 0.5*(lo+hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    return y
This bisection called only if proportional not enough. For normal small diff, no. But if z from h+direction, proportional likely enough; if diff large (random init), it will bisect 25* n. Fine.
Could we always bisection? Too slow. Good.

`project_exact(h,target)`: Use project_fast, then final one-entry:
def project_exact(h,target):
  y=project_fast(h,target)
  n=len(y)
  for _ in range(3):
    diff=target - float(np.sum(y))
    if abs(diff) < 1e-12: break
    if diff>0:
      idx=int(np.argmax(1-y))
      if 1-y[idx] <= 0: break
      y[idx]=min(1.0, y[idx]+diff)
    else:
      idx=int(np.argmax(y))
      if y[idx] <= 0: break
      y[idx]=max(0.0, y[idx]+diff)
  # final via one entry
  idx=int(np.argmax(np.minimum(y,1-y)))
  if idx>=0:
    others = float(np.sum(np.delete(y, idx)))
    val = target - others
    if 0.0 <= val <= 1.0:
       y[idx]=val
    else:
       # distribute among a few
       mid = np.argsort(np.minimum(y,1-y))[:max(1,n//2)]
       val = target - float(np.sum(np.delete(y, mid)))
       if val >= -1e-12:
          y[mid] += val / len(mid)
          y = np.clip(y,0,1)
  return y
This modifies h maybe objective. For parent, if no diff, final one-entry sets same? It sets last to target - sum(others); due float may slightly change. Could alter parent. Only do final if original diff? We can skip final if sum already close? We want exact maybe. For parent, if sum close, final adjusts one value minute; objective change tiny. But if c5 record, 1e-15 negligible. Use final only if abs(diff)>1e-10 initially? We can in project_exact compute before. But helper doesn't know. We'll make it general; minute.

`slsqp_refine(h0,n,beta,maxiter)`:
  global OPT_N, OPT_BETA
  OPT_N=int(n); OPT_BETA=float(beta)
  x0=project_fast(h0,0.5*OPT_N)
  bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*OPT_N
  constraints=({'type':'eq','fun':_constraint_fun,'jac':_constraint_jac},)
  options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'maxfun':int(2*maxiter),'ftol':1e-10}
  try:
    res=scipy.optimize.minimize(_slsqp_fun, x0, method='SLSQP', jac=_slsqp_jac, bounds=bounds, constraints=constraints, options=options)
  except Exception:
    return x0
  h=np.asarray(res.x,float)
  return project_fast(h,0.5*OPT_N)

```
def heuristic_start(n,rng):
  candidates=[]
  cand=make_random_binary_float(n,rng)
  candidates.append(cand)
  x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*2/n
  for amp,freq,phase in [(0.4,1,0),(0.4,2,0),(0.5,1,np.pi/2)]:
     h=0.5+amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*freq*x+phase)
     candidates.append(project_fast(h,n/2))
  # also parent? no
  best=None; bestsc=inf
  for h in candidates:
     sc=fft_score(h)
     if sc<bestsc: best=h; bestsc=sc
  return best
```
But function uses top-level project_fast/fft_score. No closures. Good.

Now let's verify mathematical with SLSQP: `weighted_gradient` computes grad of c_m (sum) with respect to h for weights w_m. We normalize w sum 1. For smooth G = L/β = m + log(wsum)/β where w_unnorm=exp(β(s-m)). Gradient wrt h? L = βm + log∑e^{β(s-m)}. derivative = β*dm + (β∑e^{...} ds)/∑e = β(dm + ∑w_norm ds). But dm = max s derivative? Wait s_off is a vector of all c_m dx. The max over s_off is not differentiable; but in logsumexp derivative of L/β = ∑ (e^.../∑e) ∇s, no derivative of m? Let's check if we define z=β(s-m) where m=max. The function L/β = m + (1/β) log ∑ exp(β(s-m)). Since m depends on h. Derivative = ∇m + ∑ [e^{β(s-m)}/∑e] ∇s - (m? derivative of denominator?) Let's derive: f = m + (1/β) log Z, Z=∑ e^{β(s - m)}. ∂f = ∂m + (1/β)( (∂Z)/Z ). ∂Z = ∑ e^{β(s-m)} (β ∂s - β ∂m) = β ∑ e^{...} (∂s - ∂m). So (1/β)∂Z/Z = ∑ w (∂s - ∂m) = ∑w∂s - (∑w)∂m = ∑w∂s - ∂m. Thus ∂f = ∂m - ∂m + ∑w∂s = ∑w∂s. Good! No extra. So gradient is softmax normalized weights on s. Our smooth_value_grad uses normalized w and dx*grad_c; correct. G = m + log(wsum)/β where w computed from exp(z) with z_max subtract (same as normalized?) If we use z=β(s-m), then wsum=sum exp(z). If z_max=0, wsum normal. G = m + log(wsum)/β. Good.

For max approximation, if only one active, G=m. If many active, G=m+log(N_active)/β. fine.

`projected_subgradient_refine(h0,n,deadline,rng)`:
  target=0.5*n
  h=project_fast(h0,target)
  best_h=h.copy(); best_score=fft_score(h)
  betas=[5,10,20]
  for beta in betas:
    steps=0; stall=0
    while time.time()<deadline:
      G,grad,score = smooth_data(h,n,beta)
      if score < best_score-1e-12: best_h=h.copy(); best_score=score
      gnorm=math.sqrt(float(np.dot(grad,grad)))
      if gnorm < 1e-10: break
      dir=-grad/gnorm
      improved=False
      # try alphas
      for alpha in (0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005):
         h_new=project_fast(h+alpha*dir,target)
         sc_new=fft_score(h_new)
         if sc_new < score - 1e-10:
            h=h_new; score=sc_new
            if sc_new < best_score-1e-12: best_h=h.copy(); best_score=sc_new
            improved=True; break
      if not improved:
         stall+=1
         if stall > 100:
            # add noise to best
            h=best_h + rng.normal(0,0.05,n)
            h=project_fast(h,target)
            stall=0
      steps+=1
  return project_exact(best_h,target), best_score
```
This could be slow because each failed step tries up to 5 FFT scores plus gradient (which includes two FFTs). For n=320, gradient maybe ~0.3ms, score ~0.1ms; 5=0.8ms, 10k steps=8s. Good. For n=1024, maybe 5ms*5=25ms, 4k=100s. Could use if time. But we already have SLSQP. Maybe add after SLSQP for n=base*2? Time. But code complexity moderate. `smooth_data`:
```
def smooth_data(h,n,beta):
    h = np.asarray(h,float)
    c = fft_corr(h)
    c_off = c[::-1]
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    s_off = c_off * dx
    np.maximum(s_off, 0.0, out=s_off)
    m = float(np.max(s_off))
    z = beta * (s_off - m)
    zmax = float(np.max(z))
    w = np.exp(z - zmax)
    wsum = float(np.sum(w))
    if not math.isfinite(wsum) or wsum <= 0:
        w = np.ones_like(s_off) / len(s_off)
        wsum = 1.0
    else:
        w = w / wsum
    try:
        G = m + math.log(wsum) / beta if wsum > 0 else m
    except:
        G = m
    grad = dx * weighted_gradient(h, w, n)
    return G, grad, m
```
Wait if we normalized w, wsum used in G should be original sum of exp(z-zmax) or after normalization? G should be m + (1/β) log sum exp(β(s-m)). We compute zmax = max(z)=0 if m is max, but if we do z - zmax (0). wsum original sum. Good. If we then normalize w, keep wsum_original. We store wsum from sum before normalizing. In code if wsum finite, normalize and keep wsum old? We set wsum = float(np.sum(w)) before normalization; if finite, w = w / wsum, but G uses wsum. Good. If we set wsum=1.0 fallback, G=m. Need not normalize again. But after `w = w / wsum`, wsum remains old? In code if `wsum` variable retained. Good. If fallback, wsum=1.

But we do `np.maximum(s_off,0,out=s_off)` modifies s_off; c_off is view to c reversed, so modifies c? s_off is new array from multiplication, not view, okay.

`projected_subgradient_refine`: Use `smooth_data` and `fft_score`. In line search, if alpha tries and none improves, stall. But if score is already best and no small step improves due non-smooth, noise. Could be okay. To save time, limit total steps? It uses deadline. We'll allocate reserve.
```
def projected_subgradient_refine(h0,n,deadline,rng):
    target=0.5*n
    h=project_fast(h0,target)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_score=fft_score(h)
    alphas=[0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005]
    for beta in (5.0,10.0,20.0):
        stall=0
        while time.time() < deadline:
            G,grad,score=smooth_data(h,n,beta)
            if score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_h=h.copy(); best_score=score
            gnorm=math.sqrt(float(np.dot(grad,grad)))
            if not math.isfinite(gnorm) or gnorm < 1e-12:
                break
            direction = -grad / gnorm
            improved=False
            for a in alphas:
                h_new=project_fast(h + a*direction, target)
                sc_new=fft_score(h_new)
                if sc_new < score - 1e-10:
                    h=h_new; score=sc_new
                    if sc_new < best_score - 1e-12: best_h=h.copy(); best_score=sc_new
                    improved=True; break
            if not improved:
                stall +=1
                if stall > 80:
                    h = best_h + rng.normal(0.0,0.05,h.size)
                    h=project_fast(h,target)
                    stall=0
            else:
                stall=0
    best_h=project_exact(best_h,target)
    return best_h
```
Potential infinite if `score` nan? smooth_data should finite. If gnorm nan break.

target = 0.5*best_n
if abs(float(np.sum(best_h))-target) > 1e-7:
   best_h=project_exact(best_h,target)
cfinal=exact_score(best_h) if best_n <= 2500 else fft_score(best_h)
return best_h, float(cfinal), best_n
```

```python
import time
import math
import numpy as np
import scipy.optimize as scopt

OPT_N = 0
OPT_BETA = 1.0


def next_pow2(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def exact_score(h):
    hf = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = int(hf.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return float("inf")
    c = np.correlate(hf, 1.0 - hf, mode="full")
    return float(np.max(c) * (2.0 / n))


def fft_corr(h):
    hf = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = int(hf.size)
    N = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    br = (1.0 - hf)[::-1]
    fa = np.fft.rfft(hf, N)
    fb = np.fft.rfft(br, N)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, N)
    return out[:2 * n - 1]


def fft_score(h):
    c = fft_corr(h)
    return float(np.max(c) * (2.0 / c.size // 2 * 2 - 1))  # placeholder, overwritten below


def fft_score(h):
    c = fft_corr(h)
    n = int(len(c) // 2)
    return float(np.max(c) * (2.0 / n))


def project_fast(z, target):
    y = np.clip(np.asarray(z, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    diff = target - float(np.sum(y))

    if abs(diff) > 1e-13:
        if diff > 0:
            slack = 1.0 - y
            tot = float(np.sum(slack))
            if tot > 0:
                y += (diff / tot) * slack
                y = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)
                diff = target - float(np.sum(y))
        else:
            tot = float(np.sum(y))
            if tot > 0:
                y -= (-diff / tot) * y
                y = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)
                diff = target - float(np.sum(y))

    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
        zc = np.asarray(z, dtype=float)
        lo = float(np.min(zc)) - 1.0
        hi = float(np.max(zc)) + 1.0
        for _ in range(25):
            mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
            s = float(np.sum(np.clip(zc + mid, 0.0, 1.0)))
            if s < target:
                lo = mid
            else:
                hi = mid
        y = np.clip(zc + 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)

    return y


def project_exact(h, target):
    y = project_fast(h, target)
    n = int(y.size)

    for _ in range(3):
        diff = target - float(np.sum(y))
        if abs(diff) < 1e-12:
            break
        if diff > 0:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - y))
            if 1.0 - y[idx] <= 0:
                break
            y[idx] = min(1.0, y[idx] + diff)
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(y))
            if y[idx] <= 0:
                break
            y[idx] = max(0.0, y[idx] + diff)

    idx = int(np.argmax(np.minimum(y, 1.0 - y)))
    if idx >= 0:
        others = float(np.sum(np.delete(y, idx)))
        val = target - others
        if 0.0 <= val <= 1.0:
            y[idx] = val
        else:
            slack_idx = np.argsort(np.minimum(y, 1.0 - y))[:max(1, n // 2)]
            val = target - float(np.sum(np.delete(y, slack_idx)))
            if val != 0:
                y[slack_idx] += val / float(len(slack_idx))
                y = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)

    return y


def weighted_gradient(h, w, n):
    n = int(n)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)

    fh = np.fft.rfft(h, N)

    S = np.fft.irfft(fh * np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], N), N)[:3 * n - 2]
    S = S[n - 1:2 * n - 1]

    B = np.fft.irfft(fh * np.fft.rfft(w, N), N)[:3 * n - 2]
    B = B[n - 1:2 * n - 1]

    cums = np.empty(L + 1, dtype=float)
    cums[0] = 0.0
    cums[1:] = np.cumsum(w)

    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=int)
    Wwin = cums[2 * n - 1 - idx] - cums[n - 1 - idx]

    return Wwin - S - B


def smooth_data(h, n, beta):
    hf = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    if (hf < 0.0).any() or (hf > 1.0).any():
        hf = np.clip(hf, 0.0, 1.0)

    c = fft_corr(hf)
    c_off = c[::-1]
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    s_off = c_off * dx
    np.maximum(s_off, 0.0, out=s_off)

    m = float(np.max(s_off))
    z = beta * (s_off - m)
    zmax = float(np.max(z))
    wraw = np.exp(z - zmax)
    wsum = float(np.sum(wraw))

    if (not math.isfinite(wsum)) or wsum <= 0:
        w = np.ones_like(s_off)
        w /= float(w.size)
        G = m
    else:
        w = wraw / wsum
        G = m + math.log(wsum) / beta

    grad = dx * weighted_gradient(hf, w, int(n))
    return float(G), grad, m


def make_random_binary_float(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    x = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if n >= 2:
        take = n // 2
        if take > 0:
            idx = rng.choice(n, take, replace=False)
            x[idx] = 1.0
    return x


def resample_h(h, n_target):
    if h is None:
        return None
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = int(h.size)
    n_target = int(n_target)
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    if n == n_target:
        return h.copy()
    if n_target % n == 0:
        return np.repeat(h, n_target // n)
    if n % n_target == 0:
        blocks = n // n_target
        return h.reshape(n_target, blocks).mean(axis=1)
    old_x = (np.arange(n, dtype=float) + 0.5) * (2.0 / n)
    new_x = (np.arange(n_target, dtype=float) + 0.5) * (2.0 / n_target)
    return np.interp(new_x, old_x, h)


def choose_start(n, best_h, parent_arr, rng):
    n = int(n)
    cands = []
    if best_h is not None and len(best_h) > 0:
        cands.append(resample_h(best_h, n))
    if parent_arr is not None:
        cands.append(resample_h(parent_arr, n))
    cands.append(make_random_binary_float(n, rng))

    xx = (np.arange(n, dtype=float) + 0.5) * (2.0 / n)
    cands.append(0.5 + 0.45 * np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * xx))
    cands.append(0.5 + 0.45 * np.cos(math.pi * xx))

    best = None
    bestsc = float("inf")
    for h in cands:
        if h is None:
            continue
        h = project_fast(h, 0.5 * n)
        sc = fft_score(h)
        if sc < bestsc:
            best = h
            bestsc = sc

    if best is None:
        best = project_fast(make_random_binary_float(n, rng), 0.5 * n)
    return best


def make_binary_from_continuous(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    x = (h >= 0.5).astype(np.int8).copy()
    target = n // 2
    s = int(x.sum())

    if s > target:
        ones = np.where(x == 1)[0]
        order = ones[np.argsort(h[ones])]
        for k in range(s - target):
            x[order[k]] = 0
    elif s < target:
        need = target - s
        zeros = np.where(x == 0)[0]
        order = zeros[np.argsort(h[zeros])]
        x[order[-need:]] = 1
    return x


def binary_corr_off(x):
    x32 = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.int32)
    y32 = (1 - x32).astype(np.int32)
    c = np.correlate(x32, y32, mode="full").astype(np.int32)
    return c[::-1].copy()


def binary_flip(x, y, c, n, p):
    ns = n - 1
    if x[p] == 0:
        c[ns - p:2 * ns - p + 1] += y
        c[p:n + p] -= x[::-1]
        x[p] = 1
        y[p] = 0
    else:
        c[ns - p:2 * ns - p + 1] -= y
        c[p:n + p] += x[::-1]
        x[p] = 0
        y[p] = 1
    c[ns] = 0


def binary_local_search(x0, n, deadline, rng):
    n = int(n)
    x = np.asarray(x0, dtype=np.int8).copy()
    target = n // 2
    s = int(x.sum())

    if s > target:
        ones = np.where(x == 1)[0]
        for i in ones[:s - target]:
            x[i] = 0
    elif s < target:
        zeros = np.where(x == 0)[0]
        for i in zeros[:target - s]:
            x[i] = 1

    y = (1 - x).astype(np.int8)
    c = binary_corr_off(x)
    M = int(c.max())

    best_x = x.copy()
    best_c = c.copy()
    best_M = M

    no_imp = 0
    it = 0

    while time.time() < deadline:
        it += 1

        if it < 3000:
            Tbits = 2
        elif it < 30000:
            Tbits = 1
        else:
            Tbits = 0

        p = -1
        q = -1
        found = False

        if M > 0:
            qm = int(c.argmax())
            m = qm - (n - 1)

            if m > 0:
                lim = n - m
                for _ in range(8):
                    i = int(rng.integers(0, lim))
                    if x[i] == 1 and x[i + m] == 0:
                        p = i
                        q = i + m
                        found = True
                        break
            elif m < 0:
                e = -m
                for _ in range(8):
                    i = int(rng.integers(e, n))
                    if x[i] == 1 and x[i - e] == 0:
                        p = i
                        q = i - e
                        found = True
                        break

            if not found:
                for _ in range(12):
                    p = int(rng.integers(n))
                    q = int(rng.integers(n))
                    if p != q and x[p] == 1 and x[q] == 0:
                        found = True
                        break
                if not found:
                    ones = np.where(x == 1)[0]
                    zeros = np.where(x == 0)[0]
                    if ones.size == 0 or zeros.size == 0:
                        break
                    p = int(ones[0])
                    q = int(zeros[0])
        else:
            break

        if p < 0 or p == q:
            continue

        binary_flip(x, y, c, n, p)
        binary_flip(x, y, c, n, q)
        Mtrial = int(c.max())

        accept = (
            Mtrial < M
            or Mtrial < best_M
            or (Mtrial == M and rng.random() < 0.05)
            or (Tbits > 0 and Mtrial <= M + Tbits and rng.random() < 0.05)
        )

        if accept:
            M = Mtrial
            if Mtrial < best_M:
                best_M = Mtrial
                best_x = x.copy()
                best_c = c.copy()
                no_imp = 0
            else:
                no_imp += 1
        else:
            binary_flip(x, y, c, n, p)
            binary_flip(x, y, c, n, q)
            no_imp += 1

        if no_imp > 4000 or it % 20000 == 0:
            x = best_x.copy()
            y = (1 - x).astype(np.int8)
            c = best_c.copy()
            for _ in range(30):
                ok = False
                for _ in range(5):
                    p = int(rng.integers(n))
                    q = int(rng.integers(n))
                    if p != q and x[p] != x[q]:
                        ok = True
                        break
                if not ok:
                    ones = np.where(x == 1)[0]
                    zeros = np.where(x == 0)[0]
                    if ones.size == 0 or zeros.size == 0:
                        break
                    p = int(ones[0])
                    q = int(zeros[0])
                    ok = True
                if ok:
                    binary_flip(x, y, c, n, p)
                    binary_flip(x, y, c, n, q)
            M = int(c.max())
            no_imp = 0

    return best_x.astype(float), float(best_M * 2.0 / n)


def _slsqp_fun(h):
    return smooth_data(h, OPT_N, OPT_BETA)[0]


def _slsqp_jac(h):
    return smooth_data(h, OPT_N, OPT_BETA)[1]


def _slsqp_constraint_fun(h):
    return float(np.sum(h) - 0.5 * OPT_N)


def _slsqp_constraint_jac(h):
    return np.ones((1, int(np.size(h))), dtype=float)


def slsqp_refine(h0, n, beta, maxiter):
    global OPT_N, OPT_BETA
    n = int(n)
    OPT_N = n
    OPT_BETA = float(beta)

    x0 = project_fast(h0, 0.5 * n)
    if x0.size == 0:
        return x0

    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    constraints = (
        {
            "type": "eq",
            "fun": _slsqp_constraint_fun,
            "jac": _slsqp_constraint_jac,
        },
    )
    options = {
        "maxiter": int(max(5, maxiter)),
        "maxfun": int(max(10, 3 * maxiter)),
        "ftol": 1e-9,
        "iprint": 0,
    }

    try:
        res = scopt.minimize(
            _slsqp_fun,
            x0,
            method="SLSQP",
            jac=_slsqp_jac,
            bounds=bounds,
            constraints=constraints,
            options=options,
        )
        h1 = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
    except Exception:
        h1 = x0.copy()

    return project_exact(h1, 0.5 * n)


def projected_subgradient_refine(h0, n, deadline, rng):
    n = int(n)
    target = 0.5 * n
    h = project_fast(h0, target)

    best_h = h.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(h)
    alphas = (0.10, 0.05, 0.025, 0.012, 0.006)

    for beta in (5.0, 10.0, 20.0):
        stall = 0
        while time.time() < deadline:
            G, grad, score = smooth_data(h, n, beta)
            if score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_score = score
                best_h = h.copy()

            gnorm = math.sqrt(float(np.dot(grad, grad)))
            if (not math.isfinite(gnorm)) or gnorm < 1e-12:
                break

            direction = -grad / gnorm
            improved = False

            for a in alphas:
                h_new = project_fast(h + a * direction, target)
                sc_new = fft_score(h_new)
                if sc_new < score - 1e-10:
                    h = h_new
                    score = sc_new
                    if sc_new < best_score - 1e-12:
                        best_score = sc_new
                        best_h = h.copy()
                    improved = True
                    break

            if improved:
                stall = 0
            else:
                stall += 1
                if stall > 80:
                    h = best_h + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, size=h.size)
                    h = project_fast(h, target)
                    stall = 0

    return project_exact(best_h, target)


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start = time.time()
    total = float(budget_s)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    parent = globals().get("initial_h_values", None)
    parent_arr = None
    if parent is not None:
        parr = np.asarray(parent, dtype=float)
        if parr.size > 0:
            parent_arr = parr

    if parent_arr is not None:
        h_par = np.clip(parent_arr, 0.0, 1.0).copy()
        n_par = int(h_par.size)
        target_par = 0.5 * n_par
        if abs(float(np.sum(h_par)) - target_par) > 1e-8:
            h_par = project_exact(h_par, target_par)
        best_h = h_par
        best_c = exact_score(h_par)
        best_n = n_par
    else:
        n_par = 128
        best_h = make_random_binary_float(n_par, rng)
        best_h = project_exact(best_h, 0.5 * n_par)
        best_c = exact_score(best_h)
        best_n = n_par

    if total <= 90:
        return best_h, float(best_c), int(best_n)

    deadline = start + total - 15.0
    reserve_binary = 180.0 if total >= 800 else 60.0
    reserve_subgrad = 60.0 if total >= 800 else 20.0
    soft_limit = min(deadline, start + total - 15.0 - reserve_binary - reserve_subgrad)

    if parent_arr is not None:
        p_len = int(parent_arr.size)
        candidate_ns = []
        for mult in range(1, 6):
            nn = p_len * mult
            if nn > 320:
                break
            candidate_ns.append(nn)
        if not candidate_ns:
            candidate_ns = [128]
    else:
        candidate_ns = [128, 256]

    seen = []
    for nn in candidate_ns:
        nn = int(nn)
        if nn % 2:
            nn -= 1
        if 16 <= nn <= 320 and nn not in seen:
            seen.append(nn)
    candidate_ns = sorted(seen)

    for n in candidate_ns:
        if time.time() > soft_limit:
            break

        h_start = choose_start(n, best_h, parent_arr, rng)
        c_start = fft_score(h_start)

        if n <= 80:
            base_iter = 80
        elif n <= 160:
            base_iter = 50
        else:
            base_iter = 28

        for beta_i, beta in enumerate((5.0, 10.0, 20.0, 40.0)):
            if time.time() > soft_limit:
                break
            mit = int(max(8, base_iter / (0.5 + 0.5 * (beta_i + 1))))
            h_try = slsqp_refine(h_start, n, beta, mit)
            h_try = project_exact(h_try, 0.5 * n)
            c_try = exact_score(h_try) if n <= 2500 else fft_score(h_try)

            if c_try < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_h = h_try
                best_c = float(c_try)
                best_n = n

            if c_try <= c_start + 0.005:
                h_start = h_try
                c_start = c_try

    sub_limit = min(deadline, start + total - 15.0 - reserve_binary)
    if time.time() < sub_limit - 20.0:
        if best_n < 120:
            n_sub = 160
        elif best_n < 320:
            n_sub = 2 * best_n
        else:
            n_sub = 512
        n_sub = int(n_sub)
        if n_sub % 2:
            n_sub -= 1
        n_sub = max(80, min(512, n_sub))

        h_sub = choose_start(n_sub, best_h, parent_arr, rng)
        sub_deadline = min(sub_limit, time.time() + 120.0)
        h_sub = projected_subgradient_refine(h_sub, n_sub, sub_deadline, rng)
        c_sub = exact_score(h_sub) if n_sub <= 2500 else fft_score(h_sub)

        if c_sub < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_h = h_sub
            best_c = float(c_sub)
            best_n = n_sub

    binary_deadline = min(deadline, time.time() + reserve_binary + 60.0)
    if time.time() < binary_deadline - 3.0:
        if best_n < 256:
            n_bin = 256
        else:
            n_bin = min(1024, max(256, 2 * best_n))
        n_bin = int(n_bin)
        if n_bin % 2:
            n_bin -= 1
        n_bin = max(64, min(1280, n_bin))

        h_bin = resample_h(best_h, n_bin)
        x_bin = make_binary_from_continuous(h_bin, n_bin)

        bdeadline = min(binary_deadline, time.time() + max(20.0, reserve_binary))
        x_best, _ = binary_local_search(x_bin, n_bin, bdeadline, rng)
        c_bin = exact_score(x_best) if n_bin <= 2500 else fft_score(x_best)
        if c_bin < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_h = x_best
            best_c = float(c_bin)
            best_n = n_bin

        if time.time() < binary_deadline - 12.0:
            x_rand = make_random_binary_float(n_bin, rng).astype(np.int8)
            x_rand, _ = binary_local_search(x_rand, n_bin, min(binary_deadline, time.time() + 45.0), rng)
            c_rand = exact_score(x_rand) if n_bin <= 2500 else fft_score(x_rand)
            if c_rand < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_h = x_rand
                best_c = float(c_rand)
                best_n = n_bin

    best_h = np.asarray(best_h, dtype=float)
    best_n = int(best_n)
    target = 0.5 * best_n
    if abs(float(np.sum(best_h)) - target) > 1e-8:
        best_h = project_exact(best_h, target)

    final_c = exact_score(best_h) if best_n <= 2500 else fft_score(best_h)
    return best_h, float(final_c), int(best_n)
```
